People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. They can show me all the paperwork and explanations they want, but... it doesn't change the pattern. I see it. I don't really care if anyone else does.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you affected your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No... They try to hand me paperwork or offer explanations to dismiss it, but... I analyze patterns for a living. I can see the pattern perfectly well, regardless of what they say. \n\nSo... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. Like I just said, the pattern points directly to that. They want me out of the apartment. \n\nI wouldn't be bringing up the mail or the noises if it were just a general building issue. It's... directed at me. No matter what paperwork they try to hand me to prove otherwise, it doesn't change what I see happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: It's been... about 11 months. It started shortly after I moved into the new apartment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are plotting against you in other areas of your life, outside of your apartment building?\n\nPatient: No... not that I've noticed. Just the apartment.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts that you think are true, but that others might not believe?\n\nPatient: No... I don't. I'm just dealing with the situation at my building. I don't really know what else you're looking for, but... no. There's nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your property manager and neighbors are plotting against you is something that most people would believe if they saw the same evidence?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know what most people would believe. I just know what's happening to me. It's... it's been going on for months now. The property manager and... certain people in the building. They use misdelivered mail, and... noises in the walls that are timed too perfectly to be an accident. And just... little comments from neighbors. It's all to pressure me out of the apartment. They can show me all the paperwork and explanations they want, but... it doesn't change the pattern. I see it. I don't really care if anyone else does.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you affected your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No... I manage my life just fine. When I'm not forced to deal with the apartment situation, anyway. \n\nI still handle my finances. I cook for myself, keep my appointments... I still do my consulting work, and my painting. I keep it all organized. So, no. It hasn't stopped me from taking care of my responsibilities.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No. I... I'm not mistaken. I've told you what's happening. The misdelivered mail, the... the noises in the walls, the comments. It's a clear pattern. They want me out of the apartment. Their paperwork and... whatever explanations they give, it doesn't change what I see. I'm not mistaken.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the way the property manager and neighbors are supposedly plotting against you is something that could really happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. People get pushed out of their apartments all the time. Landlords do things to... to get rid of tenants. It's a real thing. I don't really see why you'd ask if it could happen when it's... well, it's exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: It takes up... a fair amount of time. I suppose. \n\nI've had to... pull back from a few of my consulting jobs recently. There's just a lot to keep track of. I spend a good part of my week having to follow up with the neighbors, or the management office, or the building staff. Trying to get answers. \n\nSo... I guess I don't spend as much time on my normal work or my painting as I used to. I have to deal with this first.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the methods the property manager and neighbors are using to plot against you are unusual or something you've heard of happening to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... it's not unusual. I mean, harassment is harassment. People do what they have to do to make someone uncomfortable. I haven't really... surveyed other people about it. But the methods themselves, the mail, the noise... it's just what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the property manager and neighbors are using any unusual or supernatural methods to plot against you?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just... normal things. Like I said, it's just harassment.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you is the first thing on your mind when you wake up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know. I just wake up and start my day. \n\nI suppose it depends on whether there's already noise, or... if I need to check the hall. But I don't really keep track of my very first thought in the morning. I just get up and do what I need to do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates": ["F22", "F23"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

I don't really care if anyone else does.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you affected your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No... I manage my life just fine. When I'm not forced to deal with the apartment situation, anyway. \n\nI still handle my finances. I cook for myself, keep my appointments... I still do my consulting work, and my painting. I keep it all organized. So, no. It hasn't stopped me from taking care of my responsibilities.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No. I... I'm not mistaken. I've told you what's happening. The misdelivered mail, the... the noises in the walls, the comments. It's a clear pattern. They want me out of the apartment. Their paperwork and... whatever explanations they give, it doesn't change what I see. I'm not mistaken.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the way the property manager and neighbors are supposedly plotting against you is something that could really happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. People get pushed out of their apartments all the time. Landlords do things to... to get rid of tenants. It's a real thing. I don't really see why you'd ask if it could happen when it's... well, it's exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: It takes up... a fair amount of time. I suppose. \n\nI've had to... pull back from a few of my consulting jobs recently. There's just a lot to keep track of. I spend a good part of my week having to follow up with the neighbors, or the management office, or the building staff. Trying to get answers. \n\nSo... I guess I don't spend as much time on my normal work or my painting as I used to. I have to deal with this first.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the methods the property manager and neighbors are using to plot against you are unusual or something you've heard of happening to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... it's not unusual. I mean, harassment is harassment. People do what they have to do to make someone uncomfortable. I haven't really... surveyed other people about it. But the methods themselves, the mail, the noise... it's just what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the property manager and neighbors are using any unusual or supernatural methods to plot against you?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just... normal things. Like I said, it's just harassment.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you is the first thing on your mind when you wake up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know. I just wake up and start my day. \n\nI suppose it depends on whether there's already noise, or... if I need to check the hall. But I don't really keep track of my very first thought in the morning. I just get up and do what I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you dominates your thoughts throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word dominate. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's about the situation at my building. For months now, the property manager and certain people there have been... making a concerted effort to pressure me out of my apartment. \n\nJust... things like constantly misdelivered mail, noises in the walls that are clearly timed, little pointed comments from the neighbors. They try to hand me paperwork or offer explanations to dismiss it, but... I analyze patterns for a living. I can see the pattern perfectly well, regardless of what they say. \n\nSo... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. Like I just said, the pattern points directly to that. They want me out of the apartment. \n\nI wouldn't be bringing up the mail or the noises if it were just a general building issue. It's... directed at me. No matter what paperwork they try to hand me to prove otherwise, it doesn't change what I see happening.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: It's been... about 11 months. It started shortly after I moved into the new apartment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are plotting against you in other areas of your life, outside of your apartment building?\n\nPatient: No... not that I've noticed. Just the apartment.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts that you think are true, but that others might not believe?\n\nPatient: No... I don't. I'm just dealing with the situation at my building. I don't really know what else you're looking for, but... no. There's nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your property manager and neighbors are plotting against you is something that most people would believe if they saw the same evidence?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know what most people would believe. I just know what's happening to me. It's... it's been going on for months now. The property manager and... certain people in the building. They use misdelivered mail, and... noises in the walls that are timed too perfectly to be an accident. And just... little comments from neighbors. It's all to pressure me out of the apartment. They can show me all the paperwork and explanations they want, but... it doesn't change the pattern. I see it. I don't really care if anyone else does.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you affected your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No... I manage my life just fine. When I'm not forced to deal with the apartment situation, anyway. \n\nI still handle my finances. I cook for myself, keep my appointments... I still do my consulting work, and my painting. I keep it all organized. So, no. I suppose. \n\nI've had to... pull back from a few of my consulting jobs recently. There's just a lot to keep track of. I spend a good part of my week having to follow up with the neighbors, or the management office, or the building staff. Trying to get answers. \n\nSo... I guess I don't spend as much time on my normal work or my painting as I used to. I have to deal with this first.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the methods the property manager and neighbors are using to plot against you are unusual or something you've heard of happening to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... it's not unusual. I'm just dealing with the situation at my building. I don't really know what else you're looking for, but... no. There's nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your property manager and neighbors are plotting against you is something that most people would believe if they saw the same evidence?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know what most people would believe. I just know what's happening to me. It's... it's been going on for months now. The property manager and... certain people in the building. They use misdelivered mail, and... noises in the walls that are timed too perfectly to be an accident. And just... little comments from neighbors. It's all to pressure me out of the apartment. They can show me all the paperwork and explanations they want, but... it doesn't change the pattern. I see it. I don't really care if anyone else does.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you affected your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No... I manage my life just fine. When I'm not forced to deal with the apartment situation, anyway. \n\nI still handle my finances. I cook for myself, keep my appointments... I still do my consulting work, and my painting. I keep it all organized. So, no. It hasn't stopped me from taking care of my responsibilities.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No. I... I'm not mistaken. I've told you what's happening. The misdelivered mail, the... the noises in the walls, the comments. It's a clear pattern. They want me out of the apartment. Their paperwork and... whatever explanations they give, it doesn't change what I see. I'm not mistaken.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the way the property manager and neighbors are supposedly plotting against you is something that could really happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. People get pushed out of their apartments all the time. Landlords do things to... to get rid of tenants. It's a real thing. I don't really see why you'd ask if it could happen when it's... well, it's exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you on a typical day?\n\nPatient: It takes up... a fair amount of time. I suppose. \n\nI've had to... pull back from a few of my consulting jobs recently. There's just a lot to keep track of. I spend a good part of my week having to follow up with the neighbors, or the management office, or the building staff. Trying to get answers. \n\nSo... I guess I don't spend as much time on my normal work or my painting as I used to. I have to deal with this first.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the methods the property manager and neighbors are using to plot against you are unusual or something you've heard of happening to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... it's not unusual. I mean, harassment is harassment. People do what they have to do to make someone uncomfortable. I haven't really... surveyed other people about it. But the methods themselves, the mail, the noise... it's just what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the property manager and neighbors are using any unusual or supernatural methods to plot against you?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just... normal things. Like I said, it's just harassment.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you is the first thing on your mind when you wake up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know. I just wake up and start my day. \n\nI suppose it depends on whether there's already noise, or... if I need to check the hall. But I don't really keep track of my very first thought in the morning. I just get up and do what I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you dominates your thoughts throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word dominate. I have... other things to focus on. I have my work, my analysis. I'm busy with that most of the day. \n\nI only really think about it when... when they do something I have to address. Otherwise, I just mind my own business.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other thoughts that you were completely sure were true, but others did not believe?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I'm a rational person. I only deal with facts, really, so... no. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: For how many months have you believed the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I... suppose it's been about 11 months. Just... shortly after I moved into the new apartment. That's when I started noticing things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that anyone else, besides the property manager and neighbors, was out to get you or plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No... just them. I don't have any reason to think anyone else is involved. It's... strictly confined to the building. I get along perfectly fine with my colleagues at work, so... no. Just them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Like I said, it's just harassment.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you is the first thing on your mind when you wake up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know. I just get up and do what I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you dominates your thoughts throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word dominate. Otherwise, I just mind my own business.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other thoughts that you were completely sure were true, but others did not believe?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I guess I don't spend as much time on my normal work or my painting as I used to. Just... shortly after I moved into the new apartment. I spend a good part of my week having to follow up with the neighbors, or the management office, or the building staff. Trying to get answers. \n\nSo... I guess I don't spend as much time on my normal work or my painting as I used to. I have to deal with this first.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the methods the property manager and neighbors are using to plot against you are unusual or something you've heard of happening to others?\n\nPatient: I suppose... it's not unusual. I mean, harassment is harassment. People do what they have to do to make someone uncomfortable. I haven't really... surveyed other people about it. But the methods themselves, the mail, the noise... it's just what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the property manager and neighbors are using any unusual or supernatural methods to plot against you?\n\nPatient: No... nothing like that. Just... normal things. Like I said, it's just harassment.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you is the first thing on your mind when you wake up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know. I just wake up and start my day. \n\nI suppose it depends on whether there's already noise, or... if I need to check the hall. But I don't really keep track of my very first thought in the morning. I just get up and do what I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that thinking about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you dominates your thoughts throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word dominate. I have... other things to focus on. I have my work, my analysis. I'm busy with that most of the day. \n\nI only really think about it when... when they do something I have to address. Otherwise, I just mind my own business.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other thoughts that you were completely sure were true, but others did not believe?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I'm a rational person. I only deal with facts, really, so... no. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: For how many months have you believed the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I... suppose it's been about 11 months. Just... shortly after I moved into the new apartment. That's when I started noticing things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that anyone else, besides the property manager and neighbors, was out to get you or plotting against you?\n\nPatient: No... just them. I don't have any reason to think anyone else is involved. It's... strictly confined to the building. I get along perfectly fine with my colleagues at work, so... no. Just them.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you caused problems in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I... don't really discuss it with my family. So, no. But it is... time-consuming. \n\nI've actually had to pull back from a few of my consulting jobs recently. I just... I spend a large part of my week having to follow up with the building staff, the management offices, checking in on the neighbors... things like that. It's necessary, but... I haven't been able to just settle into my normal work. Or my painting routine. There's just too much to monitor right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there will ever be a time when you no longer believe the property manager and neighbors are plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I... I don't see how that could happen. Not when it's still going on. \n\nI mean... for months now, they've been doing this. The property manager, certain people in the building... they use misdelivered mail, these... timed noises in the walls. Little comments from the neighbors when I pass them in the hall. It's all designed to pressure me out of the apartment. \n\nAnd... they try to explain it away. They show me paperwork or give excuses, but... no amount of that changes the pattern. I can see what they're doing. So... no, I don't think I'll just stop believing it. Not when it's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about the property manager and neighbors plotting against you?\n\nPatient: I... I thought I just explained this. No. \n\nWhat kind of evidence would outweigh what they are actually doing? The... the misdelivered mail, the timed noises in the walls... those little comments from the neighbors. That is the real evidence. It's all just to pressure me out of the apartment. \n\nThey can give me all the explanations or paperwork they want to try and hide it, but... it doesn't change the pattern I see. So... no."
